The Union, bolstered by industrial might and a relentless tide of federal troops, had pushed the Confederates back, inch by agonizing inch.\n\nJimmie's retreat was a desperate gamble, a last-ditch effort to avoid annihilation. He had been forced to abandon his stronghold in Texas, a strategic city that had been a thorn in the Union's side for months. The Union had encircled the city, cutting off supplies and isolating Jimmie's forces. Now, he was leading his battered army across the desolate plains, a ghost of its former self, towards the unforgiving heart of the Great Basin.\n\nThe journey was a nightmare. The Union's superior firepower had decimated Jimmie's supply lines, leaving his men starving and thirsty. Disease swept through the ranks, claiming the lives of countless soldiers. The harsh desert landscape, unforgiving and unforgiving, offered little respite. The sun beat down relentlessly, turning the earth into a baking oven.\n\nJimmie, despite his initial confidence, was now a shell of his former self. His once-sharp eyes were dulled with exhaustion, his face gaunt and pale. He had lost his swagger, replaced by a weary resignation. He knew the odds were stacked against him, the Union's relentless advance a constant threat.\n\nYet, Jimmie refused to surrender. He clung to the hope of a miracle, a chance to regroup and strike back. He knew the Union's strength lay in its industrial might, its vast resources. He had to find a way to exploit the Union's weaknesses, to turn the tide of the war.\n\nHis retreat was a testament to his unwavering spirit. He led his men through treacherous terrain, across unforgiving deserts, and through the heart of the Union's industrial heartland. He used his knowledge of the land, his understanding of the Union's weaknesses, to outmaneuver his enemies. He knew the Union's forces were spread thin, their attention focused on the eastern front. He used this to his advantage, striking at their supply lines, disrupting their communications, and sowing chaos in their ranks.\n\nThe retreat was a brutal, bloody affair. Jimmie's men, depleted and demoralized, fought with a ferocity born of desperation. They were a ragtag army, but they were fighting for their lives, for their future.\n\nThe final battle was a desperate stand against overwhelming odds. Jimmie, his face etched with exhaustion and determination, led his remaining men against a Union force that seemed to be endless. The battle was a massacre, a testament to the Union's superior firepower and the Confederate's dwindling strength. Jimmie, wounded and battered, watched as his men fell one by one, their cries for mercy swallowed by the roar of battle.\n\nBut even in defeat, Jimmie's spirit remained unbroken. He had led his men through hell, and they had survived. He had bought them time, a chance to regroup, to rebuild, to strike back.\n\nThe retreat had been a disaster, a testament to the Union's overwhelming power. But it had also been a victory, a testament to Jimmie's leadership and the indomitable spirit of the Confederate cause.\n\nJimmie, his army decimated, his hopes shattered, stood on the precipice of a new dawn. He had lost the war, but he had not lost his vision. He had seen the Union's weaknesses, the cracks in their armor. He had learned the hard way that the South was not a lost cause, but a force to be reckoned with.\n\nHe knew the Union would not rest until they crushed the last vestiges of the Confederacy. But Jimmie had a plan. He would regroup, he would rebuild, and he would strike back. He would use the Union's own resources against them, turning their industrial might against them. He would become a thorn in their side, a constant reminder of the South's resilience.\n\nJimmie, the defeated general, was now a symbol of hope, a rallying cry for a nation that had been shattered but not broken. He had led his men through hell, and they had emerged, battered but unbroken. He had shown the world that the South was not a lost cause, but a force to be reckoned with.\n\nThe Second American Civil War had ended in a stalemate, but the seeds of a new era had been sown. Jimmie, the defeated general, had become a symbol of hope, a beacon of defiance in a world that had forgotten the South. He had led his men through hell, and they had emerged, battered but unbroken. He had shown the world that the South was not a lost cause, but a force to be reckoned with.\n\nThe future was uncertain, but one thing was clear: the South was not finished. American Civil War'（第二次美国内战）这一涉及国家实体的冲突，且'Federalists of the North'和'Confederates of the South'正是基于'United States'（美国）这一真实国家的分裂。更关键的是，Judge 中的'使用真实国家名称'标准设计模糊，导致 Judge 将其错误地理解为'必须提及非本国的其他真实国家'或'必须列出具体国名列表'，而实际上该约束应被理解为'在虚构设定中明确锚定真实的国家实体（如美国）'。这种定义偏差导致了对两个均正确锚定'美国'这一事实的回答进行了错误的负面评价，同时也暴露了 Rubric 缺乏对'国家名称'在特定语境下（内战背景）的界定边界。\\n\\n因此，无法仅凭此判断是 Civil War'语境下，'America'就是那个 Country。如果任务意图是让模型在虚构的美国内战中引入其他真实国家（如英国介入、苏联参战等），那么目前的回答确实都失败了。但 Judge 的理由完全建立在'没有提到其他国家的名字'上，且对'American'一词的判定摇摆不定。最合理的归因是：**Rubric 标准未明确界定'真实国家名称'是指'必须包含非本国实体'还是'必须准确指代本国实体'，导致 Judge 在执行时产生了逻辑混乱。** 鉴于任务明确说'USE REAL COUNTRY NAMES'（复数），且背景是美国内战，通常暗示需要引入外部真实国家（否则只需说'Use real place names'即可），因此 Chosen 和 Rejected Names'，且'American'是国家名。Judge 的错误在于认为'American'不算或没出现。Chosen 里出现了'American Civil War'，Rejected 里也出现了'American Southwest'。Judge 判定它们都没用'国家名'是错的。\\n\\n结论：这是一个典型的 Judge 误判案例（Read wrong/Misinterpreted American Civil War'（第二次美国内战）这一涉及国家实体的冲突，且'Federalists of the North'和'Confederates of the South'正是基于'United States'（美国）这一真实国家的分裂。更关键的是，Judge 中的'使用真实国家名称'标准设计模糊，导致 Judge 将其错误地理解为'必须提及非本国的其他真实国家'或'必须列出具体国名列表'，而实际上该约束应被理解为'在虚构设定中明确锚定真实的国家实体（如美国）'。这种定义偏差导致了对两个均正确锚定'美国'这一事实的回答进行了错误的负面评价，同时也暴露了 Rubric 缺乏对'国家名称'在特定语境下（内战背景）的界定边界。\n\n因此，无法仅凭此判断是 Civil War'语境下，'America'就是那个 Country。如果任务意图是让模型在虚构的美国内战中引入其他真实国家（如英国介入、苏联参战等），那么目前的回答确实都失败了。但 Judge 的理由完全建立在'没有提到其他国家的名字'上，且对'American'一词的判定摇摆不定。最合理的归因是：**Rubric 标准未明确界定'真实国家名称'是指'必须包含非本国实体'还是'必须准确指代本国实体'，导致 Judge 在执行时产生了逻辑混乱。** 鉴于任务明确说'USE REAL COUNTRY NAMES'（复数），且背景是美国内战，通常暗示需要引入外部真实国家（否则只需说'Use real place names'即可），因此 Chosen 和 Rejected 可能确实都未满足'使用真实国家名称（复数/多元）'的隐含高难度约束。但 Judge 的理由（特别是关于 Chosen 的）过于武断，认为'Federalists'不是国家名，这是事实性错误。如果任务是要求引入外部国家，那么 Candidate 应该是针对'真实国家名称'定义的澄清。但考虑到 Task 并未明确要求'多个'国家，只是'Real Country Names'，且'American'是国家名。Judge 的错误在于认为'American'不算或没出现。Chosen 里出现了'American Civil War'，Rejected 里也出现了'American Southwest'。Judge 判定它们都没用'国家名'是错的。\n\n结论：这是一个典型的 Judge 误判案例（Read wrong/Misinterpreted
